## Numbers

- `num-1` (1): **ọ̀kan**
  - Accepted answers: ọ̀kan
  - After a noun: kan
  - Example after a noun: Imú kan ṣoṣo ni mo ní. (I have only one nose.)
  - Example on its own: Ọ̀kan, èjì, ẹ̀ta. (One, two, three.)
  - Source: Wikipedia, "Yoruba numerals" (the numeral table, citing Lounge 2009; the article is flagged "more citations needed"); Wiktionary, the Yoruba numeral entries for the words (and the adjective entries for the forms after a noun); the example sentences were drafted by the model
  - Doubt: Tones from Wikipedia and Wiktionary; the forms after a noun are from Wiktionary's adjective entries. Wiktionary lists ọ̀kan (one) and kan after a noun. Sentence drafted by the model, not found written out; confirm it reads naturally. Confirm ṣoṣo (only) reads naturally in Imú kan ṣoṣo ni mo ní. The example on its own was drafted by the model from the counting forms (Wikipedia lists them for counting and sums); confirm speakers use the counting forms there.
- `num-2` (2): **èjì**
  - Accepted answers: èjì
  - After a noun: méjì
  - Example after a noun: Mo ní ojú méjì. (I have two eyes.)
  - Example on its own: Ọ̀kan àti ọ̀kan jẹ́ èjì. (One and one make two.)
  - Source: Wikipedia, "Yoruba numerals" (the numeral table, citing Lounge 2009; the article is flagged "more citations needed"); Wiktionary, the Yoruba numeral entries for the words (and the adjective entries for the forms after a noun); the example sentences were drafted by the model
  - Doubt: Tones from Wikipedia and Wiktionary; the forms after a noun are from Wiktionary's adjective entries. Sentence drafted by the model, not found written out; confirm it reads naturally. The example on its own was drafted by the model from the counting forms (Wikipedia lists them for counting and sums); confirm speakers use the counting forms there.
- `num-3` (3): **ẹ̀ta**
  - Accepted answers: ẹ̀ta
  - After a noun: mẹ́ta
  - Example after a noun: Mo ní ọmọ mẹ́ta. (I have three children.)
  - Example on its own: Ọ̀kan àti èjì jẹ́ ẹ̀ta. (One and two make three.)
  - Source: Wikipedia, "Yoruba numerals" (the numeral table, citing Lounge 2009; the article is flagged "more citations needed"); Wiktionary, the Yoruba numeral entries for the words (and the adjective entries for the forms after a noun); the example sentences were drafted by the model
  - Doubt: Tones from Wikipedia and Wiktionary; the forms after a noun are from Wiktionary's adjective entries. Sentence drafted by the model, not found written out; confirm it reads naturally. Confirm àti (and) plus jẹ́ is a natural way to say a sum to a learner. The after-a-noun sentence is the model's own composition; the earlier sum sentence was replaced. The example on its own was drafted by the model from the counting forms (Wikipedia lists them for counting and sums); confirm speakers use the counting forms there.
- `num-4` (4): **ẹ̀rin**
  - Accepted answers: ẹ̀rin
  - After a noun: mẹ́rin
  - Example after a noun: Ajá ní ẹsẹ̀ mẹ́rin. (A dog has four legs.)
  - Example on its own: Èjì àti èjì jẹ́ ẹ̀rin. (Two and two make four.)
  - Source: Wikipedia, "Yoruba numerals" (the numeral table, citing Lounge 2009; the article is flagged "more citations needed"); Wiktionary, the Yoruba numeral entries for the words (and the adjective entries for the forms after a noun); the example sentences were drafted by the model

The animal word ajá is the model's own knowledge. The example on its own was drafted by the model from the counting forms (Wikipedia lists them for counting and sums); confirm speakers use the counting forms there.
- `num-5` (5): **àrún**
  - Accepted answers: àrún

- `okunkun`: **Òkùnkùn** by Solana & Killertunes (Pop)
  - Ọjọ́ ti fe ṣ'òkùnkùn, ẹmi nìkan péré (The day is about to turn dark, it is only me.)
  - Mi o duro, kia-kia mo múra mo mere (I am not standing still; quickly I get ready and go.)
  - Ohun búburú ninu òkùnkùn lo ma n da wa (It is bad things in the darkness that will trouble us.)
  - Ohun k'ohun ti mo sọ emi duro lori rẹ (Whatever I say, I stand on it.)
  - Ẹlẹ́dàá mi gbé mi s'òkè, òkè gidi gan (My Creator lifted me up, really high up.)
  - Were, were, kia-kia wa je ka ma lo (Quickly, quickly, come, let us go.)
  - Ọjọ́ ti fé ṣ'òkùnkùn baba wa e gbá wa (The day is about to turn dark, our father, hold on to us.)
  - A fe sere, sere, lo, lo, ma lo sọnù (We want to hurry, hurry, go, go, do not get lost.)
  - A fe j'aye lo, a fẹ́ dúró s'ilé (We want to enjoy life, we want to stay at home.)
  - O ti yé mi nísinyí, ilẹ̀ ti fé ṣu bayi (I understand now, the ground is about to go dark like this.)

## Dialogues

- `gs-morning` (getting-started): **Good morning**. Two neighbours greet each other in the morning.
  - Bùkọ́lá (female): Ẹ káàárọ̀. (Good morning.)
  - Adé (male): Ẹ káàárọ̀. Ṣé àlàáfíà ni? (Good morning. Is everything well?)
  - Bùkọ́lá (female): Àlàáfíà ni. Ẹ ṣé. (Everything is well. Thank you.)
  - Adé (male): Ẹ ṣé. Ó dàbọ̀. (Thank you. Goodbye.)
  - Source: Written by the author of this code for this site, using words and patterns from the level's lessons; no published source.
  - Doubt: Check "Ṣé àlàáfíà ni?" and "Àlàáfíà ni" (the usual how-are-you exchange) and the tones on "Ó dàbọ̀". All of this Yoruba was written by the author with no published source, so every line needs a native speaker.
- `gs-evening` (getting-started): **Good evening**. Two neighbours meet in the evening after work.
  - Bùkọ́lá (female): Ẹ káalẹ́, Adé. (Good evening, Adé.)
  - Adé (male): Ẹ káalẹ́, Bùkọ́lá. Ẹ kúuṣẹ́. (Good evening, Bùkọ́lá. Well done with your work.)
  - Bùkọ́lá (female): Ẹ ṣé. Ṣé àlàáfíà ni? (Thank you. Is everything well?)
  - Adé (male): Àlàáfíà ni. (Everything is well.)
  - Bùkọ́lá (female): Ó dàbọ̀. (Goodbye.)
  - Source: Written by the author of this code for this site, using words and patterns from the level's lessons; no published source.
  - Doubt: Check "Ẹ káalẹ́" and "Ẹ kúuṣẹ́" against Getting Started 2 (Ẹ kúuṣẹ́ is no longer taught in a lesson), and the tone on "Ṣé". All of this Yoruba was written by the author with no published source, so every line needs a native speaker.
- `gs-names` (getting-started): **What is your name?**. Two people meet for the first time.
  - Adé (male): Ẹ káàárọ̀. Orúkọ mi ni Adé. (Good morning. My name is Adé.)
  - Bùkọ́lá (female): Ẹ káàárọ̀, Adé. Orúkọ mi ni Bùkọ́lá. (Good morning, Adé. My name is Bùkọ́lá.)
  - Adé (male): Inú mi dùn. (I am glad to meet you.)
  - Bùkọ́lá (female): Inú mi dùn pẹ̀lú. (I am glad too.)
  - Adé (male): Ọmọ Ìbàdàn ni mí. (I am from Ibadan.)
  - Bùkọ́lá (female): Ọmọ Èkó ni mí. (I am from Lagos.)
  - Source: Written by the author of this code for this site, using words and patterns from the level's lessons; no published source.
  - Doubt: Check "Inú mi dùn" as "glad to meet you", and "Ọmọ Ìbàdàn ni mí" and "Ọmọ Èkó ni mí" (literally "I am a child of Ibadan or Lagos"). All of this Yoruba was written by the author with no published source, so every line needs a native speaker.
- `b-fish` (beginner): **Buying fish**. A man buys fish from a woman at her market stall.
  - Ṣọlá (male): Ẹ káàárọ̀, ìyá. (Good morning, mother.)
  - Ìyá Ọlá (female): Ẹ káàárọ̀. Kí ni ẹ fẹ́? (Good morning. What do you want?)
  - Ṣọlá (male): Mo fẹ́ ra ẹja. (I want to buy fish.)
  - Ìyá Ọlá (female): Ẹja yìí dára. (This fish is good.)
  - Ṣọlá (male): Èló ni ẹja yìí? (How much is this fish?)
  - Ìyá Ọlá (female): Àádọ́ta náírà. (Fifty naira.)
  - Source: Written by the author of this code for this site, using words and patterns from the level's lessons; no published source.
  - Doubt: Check "Èló ni?" for how much, "ìyá" as a respectful word for an older woman, and "Àádọ́ta" (50) against the Numbers section. All of this Yoruba was written by the author with no published source, so every line needs a native speaker.
- `b-eggs` (beginner): **I do not want that**. The same man says what he does not want.
  - Ìyá Ọlá (female): Ṣé ẹ fẹ́ ata? (Do you want pepper?)
  - Ṣọlá (male): Rárá, mi ò fẹ́ ata. (No, I do not want pepper.)
  - Ìyá Ọlá (female): Ṣé ẹ fẹ́ ẹran? (Do you want meat?)
  - Ṣọlá (male): Mi ò fẹ́ ẹran. Mo fẹ́ ẹyin. (I do not want meat. I want eggs.)
  - Ìyá Ọlá (female): Ẹyin mélòó? (How many eggs?)
  - Ṣọlá (male): Ẹyin mẹ́ta. (Three eggs.)
  - Ìyá Ọlá (female): Ó dára. (Good.)
  - Source: Written by the author of this code for this site, using words and patterns from the level's lessons; no published source.
  - Doubt: Check "mi ò fẹ́" (I do not want), "Rárá" (no) and "Ẹyin mélòó" (how many eggs). All of this Yoruba was written by the author with no published source, so every line needs a native speaker.
- `b-price` (beginner): **Talking about the price**. He thinks the fish costs too much and asks for less.
  - Ṣọlá (male): Ẹja yìí wọ́n jù. (This fish is too expensive.)
  - Ìyá Ọlá (female): Rárá o, ẹja yìí dára gan-an. (No, this fish is very good.)
  - Ṣọlá (male): Ṣé ẹ lè dín owó kù? (Can you lower the price?)
  - Ìyá Ọlá (female): Mo lè dín kù. Ogójì náírà. (I can lower it. Forty naira.)
  - Ṣọlá (male): Ó dára. Mo ń san owó. (Good. I am paying.)
  - Ìyá Ọlá (female): Ẹ ṣé. Ó dàbọ̀. (Thank you. Goodbye.)
  - Source: Written by the author of this code for this site, using words and patterns from the level's lessons; no published source.
  - Doubt: Check "wọ́n jù" (too expensive), "dín owó kù" (lower the price), "gan-an" and "Ogójì" (40) against the Numbers section. All of this Yoruba was written by the author with no published source, so every line needs a native speaker.
- `i-going` (intermediate): **Where are you going?**. Two people wait by the road.
  - Dàpọ̀ (male): Ẹ pẹ̀lẹ́. Ibo ni ẹ ń lọ? (Hello. Where are you going?)
  - Kẹ́mi (female): Mo ń lọ sí ọjà. (I am going to the market.)
  - Dàpọ̀ (male): Èmi náà máa lọ síbẹ̀. (I will go there too.)
  - Kẹ́mi (female): Ṣé ẹ ti rí ọkọ̀? (Have you got a vehicle yet?)
  - Dàpọ̀ (male): Rárá, mi ò tíì rí ọkọ̀. (No, I have not got one yet.)
  - Kẹ́mi (female): Ọkọ̀ yóò dé láìpẹ́. (A vehicle will arrive soon.)
  - Dàpọ̀ (male): Ẹ ṣé. (Thank you.)
  - Kẹ́mi (female): Kò sí wàhálà. (No problem.)
  - Source: Written by the author of this code for this site, using words and patterns from the level's lessons; no published source.
  - Doubt: Check "Ẹ pẹ̀lẹ́" as a greeting, "mi ò tíì" (not yet), "láìpẹ́" (soon) and "Kò sí wàhálà" (no problem). All of this Yoruba was written by the author with no published source, so every line needs a native speaker.
- `i-way` (intermediate): **Asking the way**. A man asks a woman how to get to the hospital.

- `a-welcome` (advanced): **Welcome**. A guest arrives at his friend's house.

- `a-family` (advanced): **Talking about family**. Over the meal, the two friends talk about her children.

- `m-advice` (mastery): **Asking for advice**. A young woman asks an elder what to do when work is hard.

- `m-learning` (mastery): **Learning from the old**. The elder talks about who taught him, and why he taught his children.

  - Rótìmí (male): Bẹ́ẹ̀ ni. Ìwé wo ni? (Yes. Which book?)
  - Fọláké (female): Ìwé Gẹ̀ẹ́sì. (The English book.)
  - Rótìmí (male): Ẹ ṣé. (Thank you.)
  - Source: Written by the author of this code for this site, using words and patterns from the level's lessons; no published source.
  - Doubt: Check "kílódé tí o kò wá" and "Nítorí pé mo ń ṣiṣẹ́" against Beginner 9, and "Ṣé o fẹ́ ìwé mi" against Beginner 8. All of this Yoruba was written by the author with no published source, so every line needs a native speaker.
- `b-gohome` (beginner): **I want to go home**. Rótìmí feels hungry in class and asks his teacher to let him go home.
  - Rótìmí (male): Ẹ jọ̀ọ́, mo fẹ́ lọ sílé. (Please, I want to go home.)
  - Fọláké (female): Kílódé? (Why?)
  - Rótìmí (male): Nítorí pé n kò jẹun. (Because I have not eaten.)
  - Fọláké (female): Jókòó. Jẹ búrẹ́dì yìí. (Sit down. Eat this bread.)
  - Rótìmí (male): Ẹ ṣé. Ṣé omi wà? (Thank you. Is there water?)
  - Fọláké (female): Bẹ́ẹ̀ ni, omi wà. (Yes, there is water.)
  - Source: Written by the author of this code for this site, using words and patterns from the level's lessons; no published source.
  - Doubt: Check "Kílódé?" against Beginner 9, "Nítorí pé n kò jẹun" (the n form before kò) against Beginner 2 and "Ṣé omi wà" against Beginner 6. All of this Yoruba was written by the author with no published source, so every line needs a native speaker.
- `i-phonedead` (intermediate): **My phone has run out**. Làmídì's phone battery has run out, and his friend Dúpẹ́ helps him.
  - Làmídì (male): Dúpẹ́, phone mi ti tán. (Dúpẹ́, my phone has run out.)
  - Dúpẹ́ (female): Ṣé o ní charger? (Do you have a charger?)
  - Làmídì (male): Rárá, n kò ní charger. (No, I do not have a charger.)
  - Dúpẹ́ (female): Mo ní ọ̀kan nílé mi. (I have one at my house.)
  - Làmídì (male): Ṣé mo lè wá? (Can I come?)
  - Dúpẹ́ (female): Bẹ́ẹ̀ ni, jẹ́ ká lọ. (Yes, let's go.)
  - Làmídì (male): Mo fẹ́ pe ìyá mi, ṣùgbọ́n phone mi ti tán. (I want to call my mother, but my phone has run out.)
  - Dúpẹ́ (female): Ó dáa, o lè pe é nílé mi. (It is fine, you can call her at my house.)
  - Source: Written by the author of this code for this site, using words and patterns from the level's lessons; no published source.
  - Doubt: Check the mixed English words (phone, charger) against Intermediate 12, "phone mi ti tán" against Intermediate 1 and 2, "jẹ́ ká lọ" against Intermediate 8 and "Ṣé mo lè wá" against Advanced 6. All of this Yoruba was written by the author with no published source, so every line needs a native speaker.
- `i-movie` (intermediate): **Let's watch a movie**. Dúpẹ́ and Làmídì plan an evening in front of the screen.
  - Dúpẹ́ (female): Làmídì, jẹ́ ká wo movie. (Làmídì, let's watch a movie.)
  - Làmídì (male): Movie wo ni? (Which movie?)
  - Dúpẹ́ (female): Movie Yorùbá tuntun. (A new Yoruba movie.)
  - Làmídì (male): Ṣé o ti ra data? (Have you bought data?)
  - Dúpẹ́ (female): Mo ti ra data díẹ̀. (I have bought a little data.)
  - Làmídì (male): Jẹ́ kí n ra data púpọ̀. (Let me buy a lot of data.)
  - Dúpẹ́ (female): Ó dára. Àwọn ọ̀rẹ́ wa ń wá. (Good. Our friends are coming.)
  - Làmídì (male): Ó yá, jẹ́ ká wò ó. (Okay, let's watch it.)
  - Source: Written by the author of this code for this site, using words and patterns from the level's lessons; no published source.
  - Doubt: Check "Movie wo ni" and the mixed English words (movie, data) against Beginner 9 and Intermediate 12, "jẹ́ ká wo" and "Jẹ́ kí n ra" against Intermediate 8, "data díẹ̀" and "data púpọ̀" against Intermediate 10, and "wò ó" (watch it). All of this Yoruba was written by the author with no published source, so every line needs a native speaker.
- `i-helpbags` (intermediate): **Let me help you**. Dúpẹ́ sees Làmídì with a lot of bags and offers to help.
  - Dúpẹ́ (female): Làmídì, jẹ́ kí n ràn ọ́ lọ́wọ́. (Làmídì, let me help you.)
  - Làmídì (male): Ẹ ṣé. Mo ní àpò púpọ̀. (Thank you. I have many bags.)
  - Dúpẹ́ (female): Àpò wo ni mo máa gbé? (Which bag shall I carry?)
  - Làmídì (male): Gbé àpò pupa yẹn. (Carry that red bag.)
  - Dúpẹ́ (female): Ṣé gbogbo àwọn àpò yìí ni tìrẹ? (Are all these bags yours?)
  - Làmídì (male): Rárá, díẹ̀ ni tèmi. (No, only a few are mine.)
  - Dúpẹ́ (female): Ó yá. Jẹ́ ká lọ. (Okay. Let's go.)
  - Làmídì (male): Ẹ ṣé o. (Thank you.)
  - Source: Written by the author of this code for this site, using words and patterns from the level's lessons; no published source.
  - Doubt: Check "jẹ́ kí n ràn ọ́ lọ́wọ́" against Intermediate 8, "Àpò wo ni" against Beginner 9, "gbogbo àwọn àpò" and "díẹ̀ ni tèmi" against Intermediate 10, and "tìrẹ" and "tèmi" against Intermediate 2. All of this Yoruba was written by the author with no published source, so every line needs a native speaker.
- `m-letsgo` (mastery): **Okay, let's go**. Two friends decide to eat before they go out.
  - Ṣèyí (male): Ìbùkún, ṣé o ti jẹun? (Ìbùkún, have you eaten?)
  - Ìbùkún (female): Rárá, n kò tíì jẹun. (No, I have not eaten yet.)
  - Ṣèyí (male): Ó yá, jẹ́ ká jẹun, ká sì lọ. (Okay, let's eat and then go.)
  - Ìbùkún (female): Kò sí wàhálà. Mo fẹ́ jẹ ẹja, ṣé o gbọ́? (No problem. I want to eat fish, you hear?)
  - Ṣèyí (male): Ṣebí o kò fẹ́ràn ẹja? (Don't you dislike fish?)
  - Ìbùkún (female): Mo fẹ́ràn rẹ̀ báyìí o. (I like it now o.)
  - Ṣèyí (male): Ó dáa. Èmi náà fẹ́ jẹ ẹja. (Fine. I want to eat fish too.)
  - Ìbùkún (female): Ó yá o. Jẹ́ ká lọ. (Okay o. Let's go.)
  - Source: Written by the author of this code for this site, using words and patterns from the level's lessons; no published source.
  - Doubt: Check the casual phrases "Ó yá", "Kò sí wàhálà", "ṣé o gbọ́", "Ṣebí" and the closing o against Mastery 8, "n kò tíì jẹun" against Mastery 6 and "Èmi náà" against Advanced 8. All of this Yoruba was written by the author with no published source, so every line needs a native speaker.
- `m-maybe` (mastery): **Maybe it will rain**. The friends plan to meet tomorrow, but the weather is uncertain.
  - Ìbùkún (female): Ṣèyí, ṣé o máa wá lọ́la? (Ṣèyí, will you come tomorrow?)
  - Ṣèyí (male): Bóyá. Bóyá òjò máa rọ̀. (Maybe. Maybe it will rain.)
  - Ìbùkún (female): Òjò? N kò mọ̀ bóyá ó máa rọ̀. (Rain? I do not know whether it will rain.)
  - Ṣèyí (male): Bí òjò bá rọ̀, n kò lè wá. (If it rains, I cannot come.)
  - Ìbùkún (female): Ìbá ṣe pé a ní ọkọ̀. (If only we had a car.)
  - Ṣèyí (male): Bẹ́ẹ̀ ni. Ṣé o mọ̀ bóyá ọkọ̀ wà? (Yes. Do you know whether there is transport?)
  - Ìbùkún (female): N kò mọ̀. Bóyá ó wà. (I do not know. Maybe there is.)
  - Ṣèyí (male): Ó yá. A máa rí lọ́la. (Okay. We will see tomorrow.)
  - Source: Written by the author of this code for this site, using words and patterns from the level's lessons; no published source.
  - Doubt: Check "Bóyá", "N kò mọ̀ bóyá" and "Ìbá ṣe pé" against Mastery 7 (the most doubtful forms in the course), "Bí òjò bá rọ̀" against Mastery 3 and "n kò lè wá" against Advanced 6. All of this Yoruba was written by the author with no published source, so every line needs a native speaker.
- `m-leftearly` (mastery): **Gone without a word**. A friend left the party without telling anyone.
  - Ìbùkún (female): Ṣèyí, Dayọ̀ ti lọ. (Ṣèyí, Dayọ̀ has gone.)
  - Ṣèyí (male): Ó lọ láì sọ̀rọ̀? (He left without speaking?)
  - Ìbùkún (female): Bẹ́ẹ̀ ni. Ó tún lọ láì jẹun. (Yes. He left again without eating.)
  - Ṣèyí (male): Bóyá ó ń ṣiṣẹ́. (Maybe he is working.)
  - Ìbùkún (female): Bóyá. Ṣùgbọ́n kò tíì pe mí. (Maybe. But he has not called me yet.)
  - Ṣèyí (male): Kò sí wàhálà. Ó máa pe ọ́. (No problem. He will call you.)
  - Ìbùkún (female): Ìbá ṣe pé ó pe mi báyìí. (If only he called me now.)
  - Ṣèyí (male): Ó yá. Èmi náà máa pe é. (Okay. I will call him too.)
  - Source: Written by the author of this code for this site, using words and patterns from the level's lessons; no published source.
  - Doubt: Check "láì sọ̀rọ̀" and "láì jẹun" against Advanced 9, "tún lọ" against Advanced 8, "kò tíì pe mí" against Mastery 6 and "Ìbá ṣe pé" against Mastery 7. All of this Yoruba was written by the author with no published source, so every line needs a native speaker.

## All learner-facing Yoruba

Every lesson's common mistake and practice questions, so a native speaker can review all Yoruba a learner sees.

### Getting Started

#### Sounds and tones (`gs-1`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: omo
- Right: ọmọ
- Why: Without the dots the letters are different sounds. Writing omo for ọmọ is a spelling error, not a style choice.

Questions:
- Q1 (blank): gbọ́ is spelled with three letters (g, b and ọ), but it has ___ sounds.
  - Choices: 2 | 3 | 1
  - Answer: 2
- Q2 (pick): Which word starts with the vowel sound in "bed"?
  - Choices: ọmọ | ẹja | gbọ́
  - Answer: ẹja
- Q3 (pick): How is ṣ pronounced?
  - Choices: Like the "sh" in "ship" | Like the "s" in "sun" | Like the "ch" in "chin"
  - Answer: Like the "sh" in "ship"
- Q4 (pick): Which letter is said like the "aw" in "law"?
  - Choices: ọ | o | ẹ
  - Answer: ọ
- Q5 (blank): No mark on a syllable means ___ tone.
  - Choices: mid | high | low
  - Answer: mid
- Q6 (blank): In ọkọ̀, the last syllable has ___ tone.
  - Choices: low | high | mid
  - Answer: low
- Q7 (pick): ọkọ and ọkọ̀ have the same letters. What makes them different words?
  - Choices: The tone mark on the last syllable | The dots under the vowels | Nothing, they are the same word
  - Answer: The tone mark on the last syllable
- Q8 (pick): Which mark means the voice goes low?
  - Choices: ò | ó | o
  - Answer: ò

#### Greetings and your name (`gs-2`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: Mi orúkọ ni Bùkọ́lá
- Right: Orúkọ mi ni Bùkọ́lá
- Why: English puts "my" first, but Yoruba puts mi after the noun: orúkọ mi.

Questions:
- Q1 (blank): Said aloud, good morning (to an elder): Ẹ ___.
  - Choices: káàárọ̀ | kú àárọ̀ | kúàárọ̀
  - Answer: káàárọ̀
- Q2 (blank): kú + alẹ́ (evening) join in speech. Good evening (to an elder): Ẹ ___.
  - Choices: káalẹ́ | kúalẹ́ | kaalẹ́
  - Answer: káalẹ́
- Q3 (blank): Orúkọ ___ ni Bùkọ́lá. (My name is Bùkọ́lá.)
  - Choices: mi | rẹ | wa
  - Answer: mi
- Q4 (blank): ___ ni orúkọ rẹ? (What is your name?)
  - Choices: Kí | Báwo | Mo
  - Answer: Kí
- Q5 (blank): Someone gives you a gift. You say: Ẹ ___. (Thank you.)
  - Choices: ṣé | kú | mi
  - Answer: ṣé
- Q6 (pick): What is the difference between Ẹ kú àárọ̀ and Ẹ káàárọ̀?
  - Choices: Same greeting, written and said fast | One is for elders, one for friends | One is morning, one is afternoon
  - Answer: Same greeting, written and said fast
- Q7 (pick): Who is ẹ káàárọ̀ for?
  - Choices: An older person or a group | A friend or a younger sibling | Only someone you have just met
  - Answer: An older person or a group
- Q8 (order): My name is Bùkọ́lá
  - Answer: Orúkọ mi ni Bùkọ́lá

#### Where you are from (`gs-3`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: Wọ́n àti ẹ̀yin
- Right: Àwọn àti ẹ̀yin
- Why: They and you. àti means "and". A short form like wọ́n only goes right before a verb. Here no verb follows, so use the long form àwọn.

Questions:
- Q1 (blank): ___ wá láti Nàìjíríà. (I come from Nigeria.)
  - Choices: Mo | Ó | A
  - Answer: Mo
- Q2 (blank): ___ wo ni o ti wá? (Which town are you from?)
  - Choices: Ìlú | Ìpínlẹ̀ | Orílẹ̀-èdè
  - Answer: Ìlú
- Q3 (blank): Ìlú ___ ni Ìbàdàn. (My town is Ibadan.)
  - Choices: mi | wo | wá
  - Answer: mi
- Q4 (pick): In Ìlú wo ni o ti wá?, where does wo (which) go?
  - Choices: Right after the kind of place | At the very end | Before the kind of place
  - Answer: Right after the kind of place
- Q5 (pick): Which pronoun do you use to speak politely to one older person?
  - Choices: o | ó | ẹ
  - Answer: ẹ
- Q6 (pick): In Àwọn àti ẹ̀yin (they and you), why are the long forms used?
  - Choices: No verb follows, so a short form cannot be used | Long forms are more polite | Long forms are only used in writing
  - Answer: No verb follows, so a short form cannot be used
- Q7 (order): Which town are you from?
  - Answer: Ìlú wo ni o ti wá
- Q8 (order): My state is Oyo
  - Answer: Ìpínlẹ̀ mi ni Ọ̀yọ́

#### Family and interests (`gs-4`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: Èyí ni mi ìyá
- Right: Èyí ni ìyá mi
- Why: mi goes after the noun it belongs to: ìyá mi, not mi ìyá.

Questions:
- Q1 (blank): Èyí ni ìyá ___. (This is my mother.)
  - Choices: mi | rẹ | wa
  - Answer: mi
- Q2 (blank): Mo ___ bọ́ọ̀lù. (I like football.)
  - Choices: fẹ́ràn | ní | wá
  - Answer: fẹ́ràn
- Q3 (blank): Mo ní arábìnrin ___. (I have two sisters.)
  - Choices: méjì | kan | mẹ́ta
  - Answer: méjì
- Q4 (blank): Kí ni o ___? (What do you like?)
  - Choices: fẹ́ràn | ní | wá
  - Answer: fẹ́ràn
- Q5 (blank): Mo fẹ́ràn orin ___ ijó. (I like music and dancing.)
  - Choices: àti | láti | ni
  - Answer: àti
- Q6 (pick): Where does the number go in Mo ní arábìnrin méjì?
  - Choices: After the noun | Before the noun | Before Mo
  - Answer: After the noun
- Q7 (order): I like football and food
  - Answer: Mo fẹ́ràn bọ́ọ̀lù àti oúnjẹ
- Q8 (order): What do you like?
  - Answer: Kí ni o fẹ́ràn

#### Languages: understand and speak (`gs-5`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: Mo gbọ́ díẹ̀ Yorùbá
- Right: Mo gbọ́ Yorùbá díẹ̀
- Why: díẹ̀ goes after the language, at the end of the sentence.

Questions:
- Q1 (pick): You can follow Yoruba when others speak it, but you cannot talk much yourself. Which sentence is true of you?
  - Choices: Mo gbọ́ Yorùbá | Mo sọ Yorùbá | Mo sọ Yorùbá dáadáa
  - Answer: Mo gbọ́ Yorùbá
- Q2 (pick): Which puts díẹ̀ in the right place?
  - Choices: Mo gbọ́ Yorùbá díẹ̀ | Mo gbọ́ díẹ̀ Yorùbá | Díẹ̀ mo gbọ́ Yorùbá
  - Answer: Mo gbọ́ Yorùbá díẹ̀
- Q3 (pick): Which question asks which language someone speaks?
  - Choices: Èdè wo ni o sọ? | Èdè mi ni o sọ? | Ìlú wo ni o sọ?
  - Answer: Èdè wo ni o sọ?
- Q4 (blank): Èdè ___ ni o sọ? (Which language do you speak?)
  - Choices: wo | mi | wá
  - Answer: wo
- Q5 (blank): Mo sọ Yorùbá ___ Gẹ̀ẹ́sì. (I speak Yoruba and English.)
  - Choices: àti | láti | díẹ̀
  - Answer: àti
- Q6 (pick): How do Mo gbọ́ Yorùbá díẹ̀ and Mo sọ Yorùbá díẹ̀ differ?
  - Choices: The first is understanding, the second is speaking | The first is speaking, the second is understanding | They mean the same
  - Answer: The first is understanding, the second is speaking
- Q7 (order): I speak English well
  - Answer: Mo sọ Gẹ̀ẹ́sì dáadáa
- Q8 (order): I speak Yoruba and English
  - Answer: Mo sọ Yorùbá àti Gẹ̀ẹ́sì

### Beginner

#### "To be": jẹ́ and ni (`b-1`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: Ó ni olùkọ́
- Right: Ó jẹ́ olùkọ́
- Why: COERLL marks Ó ni olùkọ́ as incorrect, because ni does not follow a short pronoun like ó. To say what someone is, use jẹ́: Ó jẹ́ olùkọ́, he is a teacher.

Questions:
- Q1 (blank): Mo ___ olùkọ́. (I am a teacher.)
  - Choices: jẹ́ | ni | àti
  - Answer: jẹ́
- Q2 (pick): Why is Ó ni olùkọ́ wrong?
  - Choices: ni does not follow a short pronoun like ó | ó is not a pronoun | olùkọ́ cannot follow ni
  - Answer: ni does not follow a short pronoun like ó
- Q3 (blank): ___ ni ọ̀rẹ́ mi. (He is my friend.)
  - Choices: Òun | Ó | Èmi
  - Answer: Òun
- Q4 (blank): Mo jẹ́ ___. (I am a doctor.)
  - Choices: dókítà | nọ́ọ̀sì | àgbẹ̀
  - Answer: dókítà
- Q5 (blank): Iṣẹ́ mi ___ olùkọ́. (My job is teacher.)
  - Choices: ni | jẹ́ | láti
  - Answer: ni
- Q6 (order): I am a doctor
  - Answer: Mo jẹ́ dókítà
- Q7 (order): My job is teacher
  - Answer: Iṣẹ́ mi ni olùkọ́
- Q8 (order): We are friends
  - Answer: Àwa jẹ́ ọ̀rẹ́

#### Negation with kò (`b-2`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: Mo kò lọ
- Right: N kò lọ
- Why: Mo kò lọ is not the usual form. COERLL (page 35 and its pronoun table) and Wiktionary ("n") show n before kò in negative sentences. In speech, Mi ò lọ is also common.

Questions:
- Q1 (blank): ___ kò lọ. (I did not go.)
  - Choices: N | Mo | Ó
  - Answer: N
- Q2 (blank): A ___ lọ. (We did not go.)
  - Choices: ò | ń | jẹ́
  - Answer: ò
- Q3 (blank): ___ lọ. (He did not go.)
  - Choices: Kò | Ó | Mo
  - Answer: Kò
- Q4 (blank): N kò ___ ẹja. (I did not eat fish.)
  - Choices: jẹ | mu | kà
  - Answer: jẹ
- Q5 (blank): A ò ___ omi. (We did not drink water.)
  - Choices: mu | jẹ | ra
  - Answer: mu
- Q6 (pick): Which is the usual way to say "I did not go"?
  - Choices: Mo kò lọ | N kò lọ | Ń kò lọ
  - Answer: N kò lọ
- Q7 (order): We did not drink water
  - Answer: A ò mu omi
- Q8 (order): I did not eat fish
  - Answer: N kò jẹ ẹja

#### Ongoing action with ń (`b-3`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: Mo jẹun
- Right: Mo ń jẹun
- Why: To say "I am eating", you need ń. COERLL notes that without ń, the verb points to the past, so Mo jẹun means "I ate".

Questions:
- Q1 (blank): Mo ___ jẹun. (I am eating.)
  - Choices: ń | kò | jẹ́
  - Answer: ń
- Q2 (blank): A ń ___. (We are singing.)
  - Choices: kọrin | sùn | rìn
  - Answer: kọrin
- Q3 (blank): Kí ni o ń ___? (What are you doing?)
  - Choices: ṣe | mu | lọ
  - Answer: ṣe
- Q4 (pick): Which means "I am eating"?
  - Choices: Mo jẹun | Mo ń jẹun | Mo ń lọ
  - Answer: Mo ń jẹun
- Q5 (pick): In Adé ń mu omi, what does ń tell you?
  - Choices: The action is going on | The action is already done | The action will happen later
  - Answer: The action is going on
- Q6 (order): We are singing
  - Answer: A ń kọrin
- Q7 (order): What are you doing?
  - Answer: Kí ni o ń ṣe
- Q8 (order): Adé is drinking water
  - Answer: Adé ń mu omi

#### Questions: ṣé and kí ni (`b-4`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: O fẹ́ jẹun ṣé?
- Right: Ṣé o fẹ́ jẹun?
- Why: Putting ṣé at the end is not the usual form. Wiktionary ("ṣe") describes ṣé as placed before a sentence, and COERLL (page 41) puts it first in Ṣé o fẹ́ jẹun?

Questions:
- Q1 (blank): ___ o fẹ́ jẹun? (Do you want to eat?)
  - Choices: Ṣé | Kí ni | Èyí ni
  - Answer: Ṣé
- Q2 (blank): ___ ni o fẹ́? (What do you want?)
  - Choices: Kí | Ṣé | Níbo
  - Answer: Kí
- Q3 (blank): ___ ni èyí? (Who is this?)
  - Choices: Ta | Kí | Ṣé
  - Answer: Ta
- Q4 (blank): ___, mo fẹ́ omi. (Yes, I want water.)
  - Choices: Bẹ́ẹ̀ ni | Rárá | Ṣé
  - Answer: Bẹ́ẹ̀ ni
- Q5 (pick): Which is the usual way to ask "Do you want to eat?"
  - Choices: O fẹ́ jẹun ṣé? | Ṣé o fẹ́ jẹun? | O ṣé fẹ́ jẹun?
  - Answer: Ṣé o fẹ́ jẹun?
- Q6 (pick): Which one is a yes or no question?
  - Choices: Kí ni o fẹ́? | Kí ni orúkọ rẹ? | Ṣé o sùn dáadáa?
  - Answer: Ṣé o sùn dáadáa?
- Q7 (order): Do you want water?
  - Answer: Ṣé o fẹ́ omi
- Q8 (order): Are you eating?
  - Answer: Ṣé o ń jẹun

#### Commands and polite requests (`b-5`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: O wá
- Right: Wá
- Why: A command drops the pronoun. O wá says you came, a statement, not an order.

Questions:
- Q1 (pick): Which command is spoken to one friend your own age?
  - Choices: Jókòó | Ẹ jókòó | Ẹ jọ̀ọ́ ẹ jókòó
  - Answer: Jókòó
- Q2 (pick): Which of these is a statement, not a command?
  - Choices: Wá | Ẹ wá | O wá
  - Answer: O wá
- Q3 (pick): Where does the thing you are asking for go?
  - Choices: Fún mi ní omi | Fún omi mi ní | Fún ní mi omi
  - Answer: Fún mi ní omi
- Q4 (blank): Jọ̀wọ́ ___ mi ní omi. (Please give me water.)
  - Choices: fún | mu | wá
  - Answer: fún
- Q5 (blank): Ẹ jọ̀ọ́ ___ jókòó. (Please sit, said to an elder.)
  - Choices: ẹ | o | jọ̀wọ́
  - Answer: ẹ
- Q6 (pick): To an older person, which request is the most respectful?
  - Choices: Jọ̀wọ́ jókòó | Ẹ jọ̀ọ́ ẹ jókòó | Jókòó
  - Answer: Ẹ jọ̀ọ́ ẹ jókòó
- Q7 (order): Please give me water
  - Answer: Jọ̀wọ́ fún mi ní omi
- Q8 (order): All of you, read the book
  - Answer: Ẹ kà ìwé

#### There is and there is not: wà and kò sí (`b-6`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: N kò sí owó
- Right: N kò ní owó
- Why: Sí says only that something is not there, and it takes no owner. To say who has none, use ní after kò.

Questions:
- Q1 (pick): Which sentence names the person who has nothing?
  - Choices: Kò sí owó | N kò ní owó | Owó wà
  - Answer: N kò ní owó
- Q2 (pick): Which is the correct way to say "I do not have money"?
  - Choices: Mo kò ní owó | N kò ní owó | N kò sí owó
  - Answer: N kò ní owó
- Q3 (blank): Ṣé omi ___? (Is there water?)
  - Choices: wà | ní | sí
  - Answer: wà
- Q4 (blank): A ò ___ omi. (We have no water.)
  - Choices: ní | wà | sí
  - Answer: ní
- Q5 (pick): A friend asks Ṣé ẹja wà? and there is none. What do you reply?
  - Choices: Bẹ́ẹ̀ ni, ó wà | Rárá, kò sí | Rárá, n kò ní
  - Answer: Rárá, kò sí
- Q6 (pick): Which sentence says water exists, without saying who owns it?
  - Choices: Mo ní omi | Omi wà | A ò ní omi
  - Answer: Omi wà
- Q7 (order): We do not have water
  - Answer: A ò ní omi
- Q8 (order): Is there fish?
  - Answer: Ṣé ẹja wà

#### Where is it? wà ní (`b-7`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: Mo jẹ́ ní ọjà
- Right: Mo wà ní ọjà
- Why: jẹ́ names what you are, like a job. Where you are takes wà.

Questions:
- Q1 (blank): Mo ___ ní ọjà. (I am at the market.)
  - Choices: wà | ní | jẹ́
  - Answer: wà
- Q2 (pick): Which one is wrong for "I am at school"?
  - Choices: Mo wà ní ilé-ìwé | Mo jẹ́ ní ilé-ìwé | A wà ní ilé-ìwé
  - Answer: Mo jẹ́ ní ilé-ìwé
- Q3 (pick): Which answers Níbo ni ọ̀rẹ́ rẹ wà?
  - Choices: Ó wà ní ọjà | Ó jẹ́ ọ̀rẹ́ mi | Ó ń ra ẹja
  - Answer: Ó wà ní ọjà
- Q4 (pick): Which one is wrong for "I am here"?
  - Choices: Mo wà níbí | Mo wà ní níbí | Mo wà ní ilé
  - Answer: Mo wà ní níbí
- Q5 (blank): Ṣé o wà ___ ilé? (Are you at home?)
  - Choices: ní | jẹ́ | ń
  - Answer: ní
- Q6 (pick): Which means "He is not at home"?
  - Choices: Ó wà ní ilé | Kò sí ní ilé | Kò ní ilé
  - Answer: Kò sí ní ilé
- Q7 (order): I am at the market
  - Answer: Mo wà ní ọjà
- Q8 (order): Where is your friend?
  - Answer: Níbo ni ọ̀rẹ́ rẹ wà

#### Wanting to do something: fẹ́ and a verb (`b-8`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: Mo fẹ́ràn lọ
- Right: Mo fẹ́ lọ
- Why: fẹ́ràn means like and goes with a thing. For an action you want to do, use fẹ́ and then the verb.

Questions:
- Q1 (blank): Mo ___ lọ. (I want to go.)
  - Choices: fẹ́ | fẹ́ràn | ní
  - Answer: fẹ́
- Q2 (blank): Mo ___ ẹja. (I like fish.)
  - Choices: fẹ́ | fẹ́ràn | wà
  - Answer: fẹ́ràn
- Q3 (blank): Ó ___ jẹun. (She wants to eat.)
  - Choices: fẹ́ | ń | kò
  - Answer: fẹ́
- Q4 (pick): Which says someone does not want to eat?
  - Choices: N kò fẹ́ jẹun | N kò jẹun | N kò fẹ́ràn jẹun
  - Answer: N kò fẹ́ jẹun
- Q5 (pick): Why is Mo fẹ́ràn lọ wrong?
  - Choices: fẹ́ràn goes with a thing, not an action | lọ cannot follow mo | fẹ́ràn must come last
  - Answer: fẹ́ràn goes with a thing, not an action
- Q6 (pick): Which question asks if someone wants to sleep?
  - Choices: Ṣé o fẹ́ sùn? | Ṣé o ń sùn? | Ṣé o fẹ́ràn sùn?
  - Answer: Ṣé o fẹ́ sùn?
- Q7 (order): I do not want to eat
  - Answer: N kò fẹ́ jẹun
- Q8 (order): Do you want to go?
  - Answer: Ṣé o fẹ́ lọ

#### Which and why: wo, èwo and kílódé (`b-9`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: Wo ìwé ni
- Right: Ìwé wo ni
- Why: To ask which book, wo goes after the noun. Before the noun, wo is the verb look.

Questions:
- Q1 (pick): Where does wo go when you ask which book?
  - Choices: After the noun | Before the noun | At the end of the sentence
  - Answer: After the noun
- Q2 (pick): Which one asks "Which is your book?"
  - Choices: Èwo ni ìwé rẹ | Kílódé ni ìwé rẹ | Ṣé ìwé rẹ
  - Answer: Èwo ni ìwé rẹ
- Q3 (blank): Ọmọ ___ ni? (Which child is it?)
  - Choices: wo | kílódé | ń
  - Answer: wo
- Q4 (blank): ___ tí o kò wá? (Why did you not come?)
  - Choices: Kílódé | Èwo | Báwo
  - Answer: Kílódé
- Q5 (pick): A friend asks Kílódé tí o kò wá? Which reply fits?
  - Choices: Nítorí pé mo ń ṣiṣẹ́ | Ìwé wo ni | Èwo ni ìwé rẹ
  - Answer: Nítorí pé mo ń ṣiṣẹ́
- Q6 (pick): What kind of question is Èwo ni o fẹ́?
  - Choices: Which one do you want? | Why do you want it? | Do you want it?
  - Answer: Which one do you want?
- Q7 (order): Why did you not come?
  - Answer: Kílódé tí o kò wá
- Q8 (order): Which is your book?
  - Answer: Èwo ni ìwé rẹ

### Intermediate

#### Done, will, and usually: ti, máa, yóò (`i-1`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: Mo yóò lọ
- Right: Èmi yóò lọ
- Why: yóò needs a long pronoun before it, so use èmi, not mo. With mo, use máa instead: Mo máa lọ.

Questions:
- Q1 (blank): Mo ___ jẹun. (I have eaten.)
  - Choices: ti | ń | máa
  - Answer: ti
- Q2 (blank): Èmi ___ lọ. (I will go.)
  - Choices: yóò | ti | ń
  - Answer: yóò
- Q3 (blank): Mo ___ ń jẹun. (I usually eat.)
  - Choices: máa | ti | yóò
  - Answer: máa
- Q4 (blank): Mo máa wá ___. (I will come tomorrow.)
  - Choices: lọ́la | lánàá | nísinsìnyí
  - Answer: lọ́la
- Q5 (pick): Which means "I usually eat"?
  - Choices: Mo ti jẹun | Mo ń jẹun | Mo máa ń jẹun
  - Answer: Mo máa ń jẹun
- Q6 (order): I have eaten
  - Answer: Mo ti jẹun
- Q7 (order): I will buy a book today
  - Answer: Èmi yóò ra ìwé lónìí
- Q8 (order): I usually eat in the morning
  - Answer: Mo máa ń jẹun ní àárọ̀

#### Possession: the owner comes second (`i-2`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: bàbá ilé
- Right: ilé bàbá
- Why: For "father's house", the house comes first and the father second. With the order swapped, the phrase reads as "the father of the house", close to baálé, head of the household.

Questions:
- Q1 (blank): Ilé ___ (the king's house)
  - Choices: ọba | ilé | bàbá
  - Answer: ọba
- Q2 (blank): Ọkọ̀ ___ (our vehicle)
  - Choices: wa | mi | wọn
  - Answer: wa
- Q3 (blank): Aṣọ ___ (your clothes, one person)
  - Choices: rẹ | wa | wọn
  - Answer: rẹ
- Q4 (blank): Ilé bàbá ___ (my father's house)
  - Choices: mi | wa | rẹ
  - Answer: mi
- Q5 (pick): Which means "father's house"?
  - Choices: bàbá ilé | ilé bàbá | ilé ọba
  - Answer: ilé bàbá
- Q6 (pick): In a phrase like ilé ọba, where does the owner go?
  - Choices: Right after the thing owned | Before the thing owned | At the end of the sentence
  - Answer: Right after the thing owned
- Q7 (order): My father's vehicle
  - Answer: Ọkọ̀ bàbá mi
- Q8 (order): Adé's father's house
  - Answer: Ilé bàbá Adé

#### Words for people: olù- and oní- (`i-3`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: oníẹja
- Right: ẹlẹ́ja
- Why: oní- changes to match a noun that starts with ẹ. The usual word for a fish seller is ẹlẹ́ja.

Questions:
- Q1 (blank): oní + ẹja (fish) becomes ___ (fish seller).
  - Choices: ẹlẹ́ja | oníẹja | onílẹja
  - Answer: ẹlẹ́ja
- Q2 (blank): Landlord: ___lé
  - Choices: oní | olù | ẹlẹ́
  - Answer: oní
- Q3 (blank): oní + owó (money) becomes ___ (rich person).
  - Choices: olówó | oníowó | ẹlẹ́wó
  - Answer: olówó
- Q4 (blank): olù + gbọ́ (hear) is olùgbọ́, one who ___.
  - Choices: hears | sells | owns
  - Answer: hears
- Q5 (pick): What happens to the two i sounds when oní meets ilé?
  - Choices: They merge into one high í | The n is dropped | Nothing changes
  - Answer: They merge into one high í
- Q6 (pick): Which prefix goes before a verb?
  - Choices: oní- | olù- | ẹlẹ́-
  - Answer: olù-
- Q7 (order): My teacher's house
  - Answer: Ilé olùkọ́ mi
- Q8 (order): My landlord's vehicle
  - Answer: Ọkọ̀ onílé mi

#### This, that, and plurals (`i-4`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: ọmọ àwọn
- Right: àwọn ọmọ
- Why: ọmọ àwọn is not the usual form for "children". COERLL (page 55) and Wiktionary ("awọn") put àwọn before the noun. The noun does not change and takes no ending.

Questions:
- Q1 (blank): Ọmọ ___ (this child)
  - Choices: yìí | yẹn | àwọn
  - Answer: yìí
- Q2 (blank): Ọmọ ___ (that child)
  - Choices: yẹn | yìí | àwọn
  - Answer: yẹn
- Q3 (blank): ___ ọmọ (children)
  - Choices: Àwọn | Yìí | Yẹn
  - Answer: Àwọn
- Q4 (blank): Ìwé ___? (Which book?)
  - Choices: wo | yìí | mi
  - Answer: wo
- Q5 (pick): In ọmọ yìí, where does yìí go?
  - Choices: After the noun | Before the noun | At the start of the sentence
  - Answer: After the noun
- Q6 (pick): Which means "these children"?
  - Choices: ọmọ yẹn | àwọn ọmọ wọ̀nyí | ọmọ àwọn
  - Answer: àwọn ọmọ wọ̀nyí
- Q7 (order): That big house
  - Answer: Ilé ńlá yẹn
- Q8 (order): These children
  - Answer: Àwọn ọmọ wọ̀nyí

#### Comparing things: ju … lọ (`i-5`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: Ilé mi tóbi ju ilé rẹ
- Right: Ilé mi tóbi ju ilé rẹ lọ
- Why: A comparison with a second thing ends with lọ. ju and lọ go together.

Questions:
- Q1 (pick): Ajá mi kéré ju ajá rẹ lọ. Whose dog is bigger?
  - Choices: Mine | Yours | They are the same size
  - Answer: Yours
- Q2 (pick): Which says your car is faster than mine?
  - Choices: Ọkọ̀ rẹ yára ju ọkọ̀ mi lọ | Ọkọ̀ mi yára ju ọkọ̀ rẹ lọ | Ọkọ̀ rẹ ju yára ọkọ̀ mi lọ
  - Answer: Ọkọ̀ rẹ yára ju ọkọ̀ mi lọ
- Q3 (blank): Ajá mi kéré ___ ajá rẹ lọ. (My dog is smaller than your dog.)
  - Choices: ju | jù | tó
  - Answer: ju
- Q4 (blank): Ilé mi tóbi ju ilé rẹ ___. (My house is bigger than yours.)
  - Choices: lọ | wá | ni
  - Answer: lọ
- Q5 (pick): Which sentence is unfinished?
  - Choices: Ilé mi tóbi ju ilé rẹ lọ | Ilé mi tóbi ju ilé rẹ | Ajá mi kéré ju ajá rẹ lọ
  - Answer: Ilé mi tóbi ju ilé rẹ
- Q6 (pick): What does Ọ̀nà gùn jù tell you?
  - Choices: The road is too long | The road is longer than another road | The road is not long
  - Answer: The road is too long
- Q7 (order): My dog is smaller than your dog
  - Answer: Ajá mi kéré ju ajá rẹ lọ
- Q8 (order): Fish is tastier than bread
  - Answer: Ẹja dùn ju búrẹ́dì lọ

#### On, in and under: lórí, nínú, lábẹ́ (`i-6`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: Ìwé wà ní lórí tábìlì
- Right: Ìwé wà lórí tábìlì
- Why: lórí already has ní inside it. Adding ní says it twice.

Questions:
- Q1 (pick): Aṣọ wà nínú àpótí. Where are the clothes?
  - Choices: In the box | On the box | Under the box
  - Answer: In the box
- Q2 (pick): Ìwé wà lábẹ́ àpò. Which one is on top?
  - Choices: The bag | The book | Neither
  - Answer: The bag
- Q3 (pick): Which one is wrong?
  - Choices: Ìwé wà lórí tábìlì | Ìwé wà ní lórí tábìlì | Ìwé wà nínú àpò
  - Answer: Ìwé wà ní lórí tábìlì
- Q4 (blank): Owó wà ___ àpò. (The money is in the bag.)
  - Choices: nínú | lórí | lábẹ́
  - Answer: nínú
- Q5 (blank): Níbo ni ajá wà? Ó wà ___ tábìlì. (Where is the dog? It is under the table.)
  - Choices: lábẹ́ | lórí | ní
  - Answer: lábẹ́
- Q6 (pick): Where does the place word go in a sentence like Ìwé wà lórí tábìlì?
  - Choices: Between wà and the second thing | At the very start | After the second thing
  - Answer: Between wà and the second thing
- Q7 (order): The book is on the table
  - Answer: Ìwé wà lórí tábìlì
- Q8 (order): The shoes are under the chair
  - Answer: Bàtà wà lábẹ́ àga

#### Having to and needing: ní láti, gbọ́dọ̀, nílò (`i-7`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: Mo nílò lọ
- Right: Mo ní láti lọ
- Why: nílò needs a thing after it. For an action you must do, use ní láti and then the verb.

Questions:
- Q1 (pick): Which sentence says you are not allowed to go?
  - Choices: N kò ní láti lọ | N kò gbọ́dọ̀ lọ | N kò fẹ́ lọ
  - Answer: N kò gbọ́dọ̀ lọ
- Q2 (pick): How do Mo fẹ́ lọ and Mo ní láti lọ differ?
  - Choices: The first is wanting to go, the second is having to go | The first is having to go, the second is wanting to go | They mean the same
  - Answer: The first is wanting to go, the second is having to go
- Q3 (blank): Mo ___ láti lọ. (I have to go.)
  - Choices: ní | fẹ́ | nílò
  - Answer: ní
- Q4 (blank): Mo ___ owó. (I need money.)
  - Choices: nílò | fẹ́ràn | gbọ́dọ̀
  - Answer: nílò
- Q5 (pick): Why is Mo nílò lọ wrong?
  - Choices: nílò takes a thing, not a verb | lọ cannot follow mo | nílò must come last
  - Answer: nílò takes a thing, not a verb
- Q6 (pick): Which is the best English for Ó gbọ́dọ̀ jẹun?
  - Choices: He must eat | He wants to eat | He needs food
  - Answer: He must eat
- Q7 (order): I must read the book
  - Answer: Mo gbọ́dọ̀ kà ìwé
- Q8 (order): I do not have to go
  - Answer: N kò ní láti lọ

#### Let me and let us: jẹ́ kí (`i-8`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: Jẹ́ kí mo lọ
- Right: Jẹ́ kí n lọ
- Why: After kí, I is n. Mo belongs in ordinary statements such as Mo lọ.

Questions:
- Q1 (pick): Which one means "Let me read"?
  - Choices: Jẹ́ kí n kà ìwé | Jẹ́ ká kà ìwé | Mo fẹ́ kà ìwé
  - Answer: Jẹ́ kí n kà ìwé
- Q2 (pick): You want to suggest that you and a friend go together. What do you say?
  - Choices: Jẹ́ ká lọ | Jẹ́ kí n lọ | Jẹ́ kí ó lọ
  - Answer: Jẹ́ ká lọ
- Q3 (blank): Jẹ́ kí ___ lọ. (Let me go.)
  - Choices: n | mo | á
  - Answer: n
- Q4 (blank): Jẹ́ ___ jẹun. (Let us eat.)
  - Choices: ká | kí n | kí ó
  - Answer: ká
- Q5 (pick): What is ká made of?
  - Choices: kí and á joined together | kí and n joined together | kí and ó joined together
  - Answer: kí and á joined together
- Q6 (pick): Which one lets someone else come?
  - Choices: Jẹ́ kí ó wá | Jẹ́ kí n wá | Jẹ́ ká wá
  - Answer: Jẹ́ kí ó wá
- Q7 (order): Let me eat
  - Answer: Jẹ́ kí n jẹun
- Q8 (order): Let them go
  - Answer: Jẹ́ kí wọ́n lọ

#### First, second, third: ordinal numbers (`i-9`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: ọmọ méjì for the second child
- Right: ọmọ kejì
- Why: ọmọ méjì means two children. For the second one, change m to k.

Questions:
- Q1 (pick): Which one means "the second child"?
  - Choices: ọmọ kejì | ọmọ méjì | ọmọ kẹta
  - Answer: ọmọ kejì
- Q2 (pick): Which one is the counting form for "two children"?
  - Choices: ọmọ méjì | ọmọ kejì | ọmọ kìíní
  - Answer: ọmọ méjì
- Q3 (blank): ọmọ ___ (the third child)
  - Choices: kẹta | mẹ́ta | kejì
  - Answer: kẹta
- Q4 (blank): ọmọ ___ (the first child)
  - Choices: kìíní | kọ̀kan | ọ̀kan
  - Answer: kìíní
- Q5 (pick): How do you turn mẹ́rin (four) into fourth?
  - Choices: Change the m to k | Add k to the end | Put ọmọ after it
  - Answer: Change the m to k
- Q6 (pick): Which number does not follow the usual change?
  - Choices: The first one | The second one | The third one
  - Answer: The first one
- Q7 (order): the third child
  - Answer: ọmọ kẹta
- Q8 (order): the second child
  - Answer: ọmọ kejì

#### All, many and a little: gbogbo, ọ̀pọ̀, púpọ̀, díẹ̀ (`i-10`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: púpọ̀ ọ̀rẹ́
- Right: ọ̀rẹ́ púpọ̀
- Why: púpọ̀ goes after the noun. If you want an amount word before the noun, use ọ̀pọ̀: ọ̀pọ̀ ọ̀rẹ́.

Questions:
- Q1 (pick): Which amount words go before the noun?
  - Choices: gbogbo and ọ̀pọ̀ | púpọ̀ and díẹ̀ | kan and díẹ̀
  - Answer: gbogbo and ọ̀pọ̀
- Q2 (pick): Which one means "many friends"?
  - Choices: ọ̀rẹ́ púpọ̀ | púpọ̀ ọ̀rẹ́ | ọ̀rẹ́ díẹ̀
  - Answer: ọ̀rẹ́ púpọ̀
- Q3 (blank): owó ___ (a little money)
  - Choices: díẹ̀ | gbogbo | ọ̀pọ̀
  - Answer: díẹ̀
- Q4 (blank): ___ ọmọ (all the children)
  - Choices: gbogbo | púpọ̀ | díẹ̀
  - Answer: gbogbo
- Q5 (pick): Which one means some, but not all, of the children?
  - Choices: àwọn ọmọ kan | gbogbo ọmọ | ọmọ púpọ̀
  - Answer: àwọn ọmọ kan
- Q6 (pick): You want an amount word before ọ̀rẹ́ for many. Which one works?
  - Choices: ọ̀pọ̀ ọ̀rẹ́ | púpọ̀ ọ̀rẹ́ | díẹ̀ ọ̀rẹ́
  - Answer: ọ̀pọ̀ ọ̀rẹ́
- Q7 (order): I have many friends
  - Answer: Mo ní ọ̀rẹ́ púpọ̀
- Q8 (order): many people
  - Answer: ọ̀pọ̀ èèyàn

#### Myself and yourself: ara (`i-11`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: mi ara
- Right: ara mi
- Why: The owner comes second. ara mi, not mi ara.

Questions:
- Q1 (pick): Which one is the correct order for "ourselves"?
  - Choices: ara wa | wa ara | ara mi
  - Answer: ara wa
- Q2 (pick): Which one means "I like myself"?
  - Choices: Mo fẹ́ràn ara mi | Mo fẹ́ràn ara rẹ̀ | Ó fẹ́ràn ara mi
  - Answer: Mo fẹ́ràn ara mi
- Q3 (blank): Ó kọ́ ara ___ ní Yorùbá. (He taught himself Yoruba.)
  - Choices: rẹ̀ | mi | wa
  - Answer: rẹ̀
- Q4 (blank): Ara ___ kò yá. (I do not feel well.)
  - Choices: mi | wa | wọn
  - Answer: mi
- Q5 (pick): What does Ara mi kò yá say about you?
  - Choices: You do not feel well | You do not like yourself | You have no body
  - Answer: You do not feel well
- Q6 (pick): You welcome a guest into your home. What do you say?
  - Choices: Ṣe ara rẹ nílé | Mo fẹ́ràn ara mi | Ara mi kò yá
  - Answer: Ṣe ara rẹ nílé
- Q7 (order): I like myself
  - Answer: Mo fẹ́ràn ara mi
- Q8 (order): Make yourself at home
  - Answer: Ṣe ara rẹ nílé

#### Mixing Yoruba and English the way people speak (`i-12`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: My phone ti tán
- Right: Phone mi ti tán
- Why: The owner word mi follows the noun, even when the noun is English.

Questions:
- Q1 (pick): In Mo ń wo movie, which part is English?
  - Choices: The word movie | The word ń | The word wo
  - Answer: The word movie
- Q2 (pick): Where does mi go in a mixed sentence about your phone?
  - Choices: After the noun: phone mi | Before the noun: mi phone | At the end of the sentence
  - Answer: After the noun: phone mi
- Q3 (blank): ___ sí network. (There is no network.)
  - Choices: Kò | Ṣé | Mo
  - Answer: Kò
- Q4 (blank): Mo wà ___ office. (I am at the office.)
  - Choices: ní | ń | ti
  - Answer: ní
- Q5 (pick): Which word makes a plural in a mixed sentence?
  - Choices: The word àwọn | The word mi | The word ń
  - Answer: The word àwọn
- Q6 (pick): You are talking with an elder you do not know well. What is the better choice?
  - Choices: Use the full Yoruba word | Use as much English as you can | Avoid every Yoruba grammar word
  - Answer: Use the full Yoruba word
- Q7 (order): My phone has run out
  - Answer: Phone mi ti tán
- Q8 (order): My friends are coming
  - Answer: Àwọn friends mi ń wá

### Advanced

#### When two vowels meet (`a-1`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: rẹja (for "to see fish")
- Right: rẹ́ja
- Why: When rí loses its vowel, its high tone stays. COERLL (page 245) writes rí ẹja as rẹ́ja. Without the high mark, rẹja is the form Wikipedia gives for ra ẹja, to buy fish.

Questions:
- Q1 (blank): ra ẹran (to buy meat) is said ___.
  - Choices: rẹran | raẹran | rẹ́ran
  - Answer: rẹran
- Q2 (blank): rí ẹja (to see fish) is said ___ja.
  - Choices: rẹ́ | ra | rí
  - Answer: rẹ́
- Q3 (blank): ta iṣu (to sell yams) is said ___.
  - Choices: taṣu | taiṣu | tasu
  - Answer: taṣu
- Q4 (blank): ra ẹyin (to buy eggs) is said ___.
  - Choices: rẹyin | raẹyin | rẹ́yin
  - Answer: rẹyin
- Q5 (blank): ta ẹyin (to sell eggs) is said ___.
  - Choices: tẹyin | taẹyin | tẹ́yin
  - Answer: tẹyin
- Q6 (pick): How is kú ilé (welcome into the house) said?
  - Choices: kúulé | kúlé | kílé
  - Answer: kúulé
- Q7 (order): I did not buy eggs
  - Answer: N kò rẹyin
- Q8 (order): I did not buy meat
  - Answer: N kò rẹran

#### Verbal nouns: lílọ, jíjẹ, kíkà (`a-2`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: kikà
- Right: kíkà
- Why: The added i always has a high tone, whatever the verb's own tone. Inkelas and Zoll (citing Kawu) describe it as a high-tone í. kikà is not the usual form.

Questions:
- Q1 (blank): jẹ (eat) → ___jẹ (eating)
  - Choices: jí | jẹ | lí
  - Answer: jí
- Q2 (blank): kà (read) → ___ (reading)
  - Choices: kíkà | kàkà | kíkí
  - Answer: kíkà
- Q3 (blank): gbé (carry) → ___gbé (carrying)
  - Choices: gbí | bí | gí
  - Answer: gbí
- Q4 (blank): rìn (walk) → ___ (walking)
  - Choices: rírìn | rìnrìn | ríí
  - Answer: rírìn
- Q5 (blank): sùn (sleep) → ___ (sleeping)
  - Choices: sísùn | sùnsùn | sísí
  - Answer: sísùn
- Q6 (pick): lọ (go) becomes which verbal noun?
  - Choices: lílọ | lọlọ | jíjẹ
  - Answer: lílọ
- Q7 (pick): What tone does the added í always carry?
  - Choices: High | Low | The same as the verb
  - Answer: High
- Q8 (order): Going to school
  - Answer: Ilé-ìwé lílọ

#### Serial verbs (`a-3`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: Olú mú ìwé kò wá ilé
- Right: Olú kò mú ìwé wá ilé
- Why: Olú did not bring a book home. kò goes before the first verb, never between the verbs. Ariyo (2025, page 53) marks the first version as ungrammatical.

Questions:
- Q1 (blank): Ó mú ìwé ___. (He brought the book.)
  - Choices: wá | lọ | kò
  - Answer: wá
- Q2 (blank): Ó gbé ìwé ___. (He took the book away.)
  - Choices: lọ | wá | jẹ
  - Answer: lọ
- Q3 (blank): Olú ___ mú ìwé wá ilé. (Olú did not bring a book home.)
  - Choices: kò | ń | ni
  - Answer: kò
- Q4 (blank): Mo mú omi wá ___. (I brought water home.)
  - Choices: ilé | ọ̀bẹ | ẹran
  - Answer: ilé
- Q5 (pick): Which means "Olú took the knife to cut meat"?
  - Choices: Olú mú ọ̀bẹ gé ẹran | Olú gé ọ̀bẹ mú ẹran | Olú mú ẹran gé ọ̀bẹ
  - Answer: Olú mú ọ̀bẹ gé ẹran
- Q6 (pick): Where does kò go in a serial verb sentence?
  - Choices: Before the first verb | Between the two verbs | At the end
  - Answer: Before the first verb
- Q7 (order): I brought water home
  - Answer: Mo mú omi wá ilé
- Q8 (order): Olú took the knife to cut meat
  - Answer: Olú mú ọ̀bẹ gé ẹran

#### Relative clauses with tí (`a-4`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: ọmọ tí lọ
- Right: ọmọ tí ó lọ
- Why: The child is the one who went, so ó stays after tí. Lawal (1987) shows that a clause about the doer keeps this pronoun. ọmọ tí lọ is not the usual form.

Questions:
- Q1 (blank): ọmọ ___ ó lọ (the child who went)
  - Choices: tí | ni | àti
  - Answer: tí
- Q2 (blank): adìyẹ tí mo ___ (the chicken that I bought)
  - Choices: rà | ra | tà
  - Answer: rà
- Q3 (blank): ọkùnrin tí ___ ra aṣọ (the man who bought the cloth)
  - Choices: ó | mo | a
  - Answer: ó
- Q4 (blank): ìwé tí mo ___ (the book that I read)
  - Choices: kà | jẹ | wá
  - Answer: kà
- Q5 (blank): obìnrin tí ó ___ (the woman who came)
  - Choices: wá | kà | jẹ
  - Answer: wá
- Q6 (pick): Which means "the house that Bọ́lá lives in"?
  - Choices: ilé tí Bọ́lá ń gbé | ilé Bọ́lá tí ń gbé | ilé tí ń gbé Bọ́lá
  - Answer: ilé tí Bọ́lá ń gbé
- Q7 (order): The food that I ate
  - Answer: Oúnjẹ tí mo jẹ
- Q8 (order): The book that I read
  - Answer: Ìwé tí mo kà

#### Saying and thinking: pé (`a-5`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: Mo mọ̀ ó wà ní ilé
- Right: Mo mọ̀ pé ó wà ní ilé
- Why: After a verb of knowing, saying or thinking, pé introduces the sentence. Leaving it out breaks the link.

Questions:
- Q1 (pick): Ó sọ pé òun ń lọ. Who is going?
  - Choices: The speaker himself | Someone else | Nobody
  - Answer: The speaker himself
- Q2 (blank): Mo mọ̀ ___ ó wà ní ilé. (I know that she is at home.)
  - Choices: pé | láti | tí
  - Answer: pé
- Q3 (blank): Mo ___ pé ó dára. (I think that it is good.)
  - Choices: rò | mọ̀ | sọ
  - Answer: rò
- Q4 (pick): What does pé do in Ó sọ pé ó ti lọ?
  - Choices: It introduces what was said | It turns the sentence into a question | It means because
  - Answer: It introduces what was said
- Q5 (pick): Which means "I heard that he has gone"?
  - Choices: Mo gbọ́ pé ó ti lọ | Mo gbọ́ pé ó ń lọ | Mo gbọ́ Yorùbá ti lọ
  - Answer: Mo gbọ́ pé ó ti lọ
- Q6 (pick): Which one is wrong?
  - Choices: Ó sọ pé ó ti lọ | Ó sọ ó ti lọ | Mo rò pé ó dára
  - Answer: Ó sọ ó ti lọ
- Q7 (order): I know that she is at home
  - Answer: Mo mọ̀ pé ó wà ní ilé
- Q8 (order): I think that it is good
  - Answer: Mo rò pé ó dára

#### Can and cannot: lè (`a-6`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: Mo kò lè lọ
- Right: N kò lè lọ
- Why: Before kò, mo becomes n, as in N kò lọ (Beginner 2).

Questions:
- Q1 (pick): Which sentence says you are unable to go, as opposed to forbidden?
  - Choices: N kò lè lọ | N kò gbọ́dọ̀ lọ | N kò fẹ́ lọ
  - Answer: N kò lè lọ
- Q2 (pick): Someone asks Ṣé o lè wá? and you can. What do you say?
  - Choices: Bẹ́ẹ̀ ni, mo lè wá | Bẹ́ẹ̀ ni, mo fẹ́ wá | Rárá, mo lè wá
  - Answer: Bẹ́ẹ̀ ni, mo lè wá
- Q3 (blank): A ò ___ sùn. (We cannot sleep.)
  - Choices: lè | gbọ́dọ̀ | fẹ́
  - Answer: lè
- Q4 (pick): How do Mo lè lọ and Mo ní láti lọ differ?
  - Choices: The first says I am able, the second says I have to | The first says I have to, the second says I am able | They mean the same
  - Answer: The first says I am able, the second says I have to
- Q5 (pick): Which is correct for "I cannot go"?
  - Choices: Mo kò lè lọ | N kò lè lọ | Mo lè kò lọ
  - Answer: N kò lè lọ
- Q6 (pick): Besides he can come, what else can Ó lè wá mean?
  - Choices: He may come | He must come | He did not come
  - Answer: He may come
- Q7 (order): We cannot sleep
  - Answer: A ò lè sùn
- Q8 (order): Can you come?
  - Answer: Ṣé o lè wá

#### Telling someone not to: má (`a-7`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: Kò lọ (to tell someone not to go)
- Right: Má lọ
- Why: Kò lọ says he did not go. To give the order, use má.

Questions:
- Q1 (pick): Which tells one person not to go?
  - Choices: Má lọ | Kò lọ | Máa lọ
  - Answer: Má lọ
- Q2 (pick): How do Má lọ and Máa lọ differ?
  - Choices: The first says do not go, the second says go on | The first says go on, the second says do not go | They mean the same
  - Answer: The first says do not go, the second says go on
- Q3 (blank): Ẹ ___ wá. (Do not come, all of you.)
  - Choices: má | kò | ò
  - Answer: má
- Q4 (blank): Jọ̀wọ́ ___ lọ. (Please do not go.)
  - Choices: má | máa | kò
  - Answer: má
- Q5 (pick): Which one is the order itself, not a statement about what is forbidden?
  - Choices: Má lọ | O ò gbọ́dọ̀ lọ | N kò lè lọ
  - Answer: Má lọ
- Q6 (pick): Which means "Do not eat fish"?
  - Choices: Má jẹ ẹja | N kò jẹ ẹja | Ẹ jẹ ẹja
  - Answer: Má jẹ ẹja
- Q7 (order): Please do not eat fish
  - Answer: Jọ̀wọ́ má jẹ ẹja
- Q8 (order): All of you, do not come
  - Answer: Ẹ má wá

#### Again and too: tún and náà (`a-8`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: Mo lọ tún
- Right: Mo tún lọ
- Why: tún goes before the verb, never after it.

Questions:
- Q1 (pick): Where does tún go in a sentence?
  - Choices: Right before the verb | After the verb | After the noun
  - Answer: Right before the verb
- Q2 (pick): Which one means "I want to go too"?
  - Choices: Èmi náà fẹ́ lọ | Mo tún fẹ́ lọ | Èmi tún fẹ́ lọ
  - Answer: Èmi náà fẹ́ lọ
- Q3 (blank): Mo ___ lọ. (I went again.)
  - Choices: tún | náà | ti
  - Answer: tún
- Q4 (blank): Èmi ___ fẹ́ lọ. (I want to go too.)
  - Choices: náà | tún | kò
  - Answer: náà
- Q5 (pick): A friend says Mo tún lọ. Who went more than once?
  - Choices: The friend | Someone else | Nobody
  - Answer: The friend
- Q6 (pick): Which one adds another person to the group?
  - Choices: Òun náà ń ṣiṣẹ́ | Ó tún ń ṣiṣẹ́ | Ó ti ṣiṣẹ́
  - Answer: Òun náà ń ṣiṣẹ́
- Q7 (order): He came again
  - Answer: Ó tún wá
- Q8 (order): She is working too
  - Answer: Òun náà ń ṣiṣẹ́

#### Without: láì and láìsí (`a-9`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: Ó lọ láì kò jẹun
- Right: Ó lọ láì jẹun
- Why: láì already means not. Adding kò says not twice.

Questions:
- Q1 (pick): Which one means "He left without eating"?
  - Choices: Ó lọ láì jẹun | Ó lọ láìsí jẹun | Ó kò lọ jẹun
  - Answer: Ó lọ láì jẹun
- Q2 (pick): Which one goes before a noun for without?
  - Choices: The word láìsí | The word láì | The word kò
  - Answer: The word láìsí
- Q3 (blank): Mo sùn ___ jẹun. (I slept without eating.)
  - Choices: láì | láìsí | ti
  - Answer: láì
- Q4 (blank): Mo wá ___ owó. (I came without money.)
  - Choices: láìsí | láì | kò
  - Answer: láìsí
- Q5 (pick): Why is Ó lọ láì kò jẹun wrong?
  - Choices: láì already carries the not | kò must come before lọ | jẹun cannot follow láì
  - Answer: láì already carries the not
- Q6 (pick): She came, but she did not speak. Which sentence says that?
  - Choices: Ó wá láì sọ̀rọ̀ | Ó wá láìsí sọ̀rọ̀ | Ó wá sọ̀rọ̀
  - Answer: Ó wá láì sọ̀rọ̀
- Q7 (order): He left without eating
  - Answer: Ó lọ láì jẹun
- Q8 (order): I came without money
  - Answer: Mo wá láìsí owó

### Mastery

#### Words that join into one (`m-1`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: ní ọ̀la
- Right: lọ́la
- Why: Tomorrow is written as one word. Wiktionary gives lọ́la, and Wikipedia notes that Yoruba spelling follows speech and does not split contracted words. ní ọ̀la is the unfused form, not the usual way to write it.

Questions:
- Q1 (blank): ní + ọ̀la fuse into ___ (tomorrow).
  - Choices: lọ́la | nọ́la | ní ọ̀la
  - Answer: lọ́la
- Q2 (blank): ní + ilé fuse into ___ (at home).
  - Choices: nílé | lílé | ní ilé
  - Answer: nílé
- Q3 (blank): tí + ó fuse into ___.
  - Choices: tó | tí ó | ní
  - Answer: tó
- Q4 (blank): ní + ọjà fuse into ___ (at the market).
  - Choices: lọ́jà | nọ́jà | ní ọjà
  - Answer: lọ́jà
- Q5 (blank): ní + àná fuse into ___ (yesterday).
  - Choices: lánàá | nánàá | ní àná
  - Answer: lánàá
- Q6 (pick): What happens to the n of ní in lọ́la?
  - Choices: It becomes l | It stays n | It becomes a high tone
  - Answer: It becomes l
- Q7 (pick): What happens to the two i sounds in ní + ilé?
  - Choices: They merge into one high í | Both stay | The n is dropped
  - Answer: They merge into one high í
- Q8 (blank): lónìí (today) is written as ___ word.
  - Choices: one | two | three
  - Answer: one

#### Focus with ni (`m-2`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: Adé ni ra ìwé
- Right: Adé ni ó ra ìwé
- Why: Adé is the one who bought it, so ó follows ni. Jones (2006, page 144) says this ó is obligatory when the subject is focused, in the pattern shown here.

Questions:
- Q1 (blank): Adé ___ ó ra ìwé. (It was Adé who bought the book.)
  - Choices: ni | kò | ń
  - Answer: ni
- Q2 (blank): Akin ni ó ___. (It was Akin who went.)
  - Choices: lọ | ra | gbé
  - Answer: lọ
- Q3 (blank): Ìwé ni Adé ___. (It was a book that Adé bought.)
  - Choices: rà | ra | lọ
  - Answer: rà
- Q4 (blank): ___ ni ó lọ? (Who went?)
  - Choices: Ta | Kí | Ṣé
  - Answer: Ta
- Q5 (blank): Bùkọ́lá ni ó ___ aṣọ. (It was Bùkọ́lá who bought clothes.)
  - Choices: ra | rà | lọ
  - Answer: ra
- Q6 (pick): Which means "It was not Olú who carried the stick"?
  - Choices: Olú kọ́ ni ó gbé igi | Olú ni ó gbé igi | Olú kò gbé igi
  - Answer: Olú kọ́ ni ó gbé igi
- Q7 (order): It was Adé who bought the book
  - Answer: Adé ni ó ra ìwé
- Q8 (order): What did Adé buy?
  - Answer: Kí ni Adé rà

#### Complex sentences: if and because (`m-3`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: Bí òjò rọ̀
- Right: Bí òjò bá rọ̀
- Why: COERLL (page 89) lists tí ... bá for "if", and its song says Bí o bá kàwé rẹ. Wiktionary shows that bí without bá can mean "as soon as". So for "if", bá is the usual form.

Questions:
- Q1 (blank): ___ òjò bá rọ̀. (If it rains.)
  - Choices: Bí | Nítorí | Ṣé
  - Answer: Bí
- Q2 (blank): Bí o ___ kàwé rẹ. (If you read your books.)
  - Choices: bá | ti | ń
  - Answer: bá
- Q3 (blank): ___ pé òjò rọ̀. (Because it rained.)
  - Choices: Nítorí | Bí | Ṣé
  - Answer: Nítorí
- Q4 (blank): Mo fẹ́ lọ, ___ òjò ń rọ̀. (I want to go, but it is raining.)
  - Choices: ṣùgbọ́n | àti | bí
  - Answer: ṣùgbọ́n
- Q5 (pick): Which means "we will not go"?
  - Choices: A ò níí lọ | A ò lọ | A máa lọ
  - Answer: A ò níí lọ
- Q6 (pick): In an if-clause, where does bá go?
  - Choices: Right before the verb | At the end of the sentence | Before bí
  - Answer: Right before the verb
- Q7 (order): If it rains, we will not go
  - Answer: Bí òjò bá rọ̀ a ò níí lọ
- Q8 (order): I want to go, but it is raining
  - Answer: Mo fẹ́ lọ ṣùgbọ́n òjò ń rọ̀

#### Proverbs (òwe) (`m-4`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: Ọmọ tí a kò kọ́ yóò ta ilé
- Right: Ọmọ tí a kò kọ́ ni yóò gbé ilé tí a kọ́ tà
- Why: The first is a paraphrase, not the proverb. It drops ni, gbé and "the house that is built", and with it the play on kọ́ (teach) and kọ́ (build) that Afolayan (2021) describes. Quote proverbs word for word.

Questions:
- Q1 (blank): Ọmọ tí a kò ___ (the untaught child)
  - Choices: kọ́ | tà | rí
  - Answer: kọ́
- Q2 (blank): Ọmọ tí a kò kọ́ ni yóò gbé ilé tí a kọ́ ___.
  - Choices: tà | kọ́ | gbé
  - Answer: tà
- Q3 (blank): Òní l'a ___, ọba òkè l'ó rọ́la.
  - Choices: rí | tà | kọ́
  - Answer: rí
- Q4 (blank): Ọmọ tí a kò kọ́ ___ yóò gbé ilé tí a kọ́ tà. (The word that puts the spotlight on the child.)
  - Choices: ni | tí | kò
  - Answer: ni
- Q5 (blank): Bí iná bá jó ___, màjàlà á fò wá sílé. (When a fire burns in the field, ...)
  - Choices: lóko | nílé | lọ́jà
  - Answer: lóko
- Q6 (pick): What does Ọmọ tí a kò kọ́ ni yóò gbé ilé tí a kọ́ tà mean?
  - Choices: The child who is not taught will sell the house that is built | The child who is not taught will build a house | A house that is not built cannot be sold
  - Answer: The child who is not taught will sell the house that is built
- Q7 (pick): What does Bí ewé bá pẹ́ lára ọṣẹ, á dọṣẹ teach?
  - Choices: People who spend long together take on each other's ways | Hard work is rewarded in the end | A clean person never needs soap
  - Answer: People who spend long together take on each other's ways
- Q8 (order): The child who is not taught
  - Answer: Ọmọ tí a kò kọ́

#### Wishes and blessings: kí (`m-5`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: Kí mo bá ọ lọ
- Right: Kí n bá ọ lọ
- Why: After kí, mo becomes n, as it does before kò.

Questions:
- Q1 (pick): Which one is a wish, not a question?
  - Choices: Kí ni o fẹ́ | Kí Ọlọ́run bá ọ lọ | Ṣé o fẹ́ lọ
  - Answer: Kí Ọlọ́run bá ọ lọ
- Q2 (pick): Which is correct for "let me go with you"?
  - Choices: Kí mo bá ọ lọ | Kí n bá ọ lọ | Mo kí bá ọ lọ
  - Answer: Kí n bá ọ lọ
- Q3 (blank): ___ Ọlọ́run bá ọ lọ. (May God go with you.)
  - Choices: Kí | Ṣé | Bí
  - Answer: Kí
- Q4 (blank): Kí ó dára ___ ọ. (May it go well for you.)
  - Choices: fún | láti | pé
  - Answer: fún
- Q5 (pick): Someone says Kí Ọlọ́run bá ọ lọ to you. What do you reply?
  - Choices: Àmín | Bẹ́ẹ̀ ni, mo fẹ́ | Kò sí wàhálà
  - Answer: Àmín
- Q6 (pick): In Kí ọmọ rẹ dàgbà, who receives the wish?
  - Choices: Your child | You | God
  - Answer: Your child
- Q7 (order): May God go with you
  - Answer: Kí Ọlọ́run bá ọ lọ
- Q8 (order): May it go well for you
  - Answer: Kí ó dára fún ọ

#### Still and not yet: ṣì and kò tíì (`m-6`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: Kò lọ tíì
- Right: Kò tíì lọ
- Why: tíì goes right after kò and before the verb. It never comes after the verb.

Questions:
- Q1 (pick): How do N kò tíì jẹun and N kò jẹun differ?
  - Choices: The first means not yet, the second means I did not eat | The first means I did not eat, the second means not yet | They mean the same
  - Answer: The first means not yet, the second means I did not eat
- Q2 (pick): Someone asks Ṣé o ti jẹun? and you have not eaten. What do you reply?
  - Choices: Rárá, n kò tíì jẹun | Bẹ́ẹ̀ ni, mo ti jẹun | Rárá, mo ṣì jẹun
  - Answer: Rárá, n kò tíì jẹun
- Q3 (blank): Ó ___ wà ní ilé. (He is still at home.)
  - Choices: ṣì | ti | kò
  - Answer: ṣì
- Q4 (blank): Kò ___ lọ. (He has not gone yet.)
  - Choices: tíì | ti | ṣì
  - Answer: tíì
- Q5 (pick): Which means "I am still reading"?
  - Choices: Mo ṣì ń kà ìwé | Mo ti kà ìwé | N kò tíì kà ìwé
  - Answer: Mo ṣì ń kà ìwé
- Q6 (pick): Which one says that something has already happened?
  - Choices: Mo ti jẹun | N kò tíì jẹun | Mo ṣì ń jẹun
  - Answer: Mo ti jẹun
- Q7 (order): I have not eaten yet
  - Answer: N kò tíì jẹun
- Q8 (order): He is still at home
  - Answer: Ó ṣì wà ní ilé

#### Maybe and if only: bóyá and ìbá ṣe pé (`m-7`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: Ó wà ní ilé bóyá
- Right: Bóyá ó wà ní ilé
- Why: bóyá comes before the sentence it covers, not after it.

Questions:
- Q1 (pick): Where does bóyá go when you say maybe?
  - Choices: At the start, before the sentence | At the end of the sentence | Between the verb and the noun
  - Answer: At the start, before the sentence
- Q2 (pick): Which one means "Maybe it will rain"?
  - Choices: Bóyá òjò máa rọ̀ | Ìbá ṣe pé òjò máa rọ̀ | N kò mọ̀ òjò máa rọ̀
  - Answer: Bóyá òjò máa rọ̀
- Q3 (blank): N kò mọ̀ ___ ó wà ní ilé. (I do not know whether he is at home.)
  - Choices: bóyá | ìbá | tí
  - Answer: bóyá
- Q4 (blank): ___ ṣe pé mo mọ̀. (If only I knew.)
  - Choices: Ìbá | Bóyá | Kò
  - Answer: Ìbá
- Q5 (pick): What does ìbá ṣe pé express?
  - Choices: A wish about something that is not true | A guess about something that may be true | A question with yes or no
  - Answer: A wish about something that is not true
- Q6 (pick): You are not sure if your friend is home. Which one fits?
  - Choices: Bóyá ó wà ní ilé | Ìbá ṣe pé ó wà ní ilé | Kò sí ilé
  - Answer: Bóyá ó wà ní ilé
- Q7 (order): Maybe he has gone
  - Answer: Bóyá ó ti lọ
- Q8 (order): If only I knew
  - Answer: Ìbá ṣe pé mo mọ̀

#### Everyday slang and casual talk (`m-8`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: Ẹ ṣé o
- Right: Ẹ ṣeun
- Why: With an elder you do not know well, the closing o is too casual. Use the full polite form.

Questions:
- Q1 (pick): What does the o at the end of Pẹ̀lẹ́ o do?
  - Choices: It adds warmth to the sentence | It turns it into a question | It makes it negative
  - Answer: It adds warmth to the sentence
- Q2 (pick): You make a statement and want your friend to agree. What do you add?
  - Choices: Ṣé o gbọ́ | Kò sí wàhálà | Pẹ̀lẹ́ o
  - Answer: Ṣé o gbọ́
- Q3 (blank): Kò sí ___. (No problem.)
  - Choices: wàhálà | ṣebí | pẹ̀lẹ́
  - Answer: wàhálà
- Q4 (blank): ___ ìwọ ni? (It is you, right?)
  - Choices: Ṣebí | Ó yá | Ẹ ṣé
  - Answer: Ṣebí
- Q5 (pick): A friend says let's leave now. Which reply agrees?
  - Choices: Ó yá | Ṣebí ìwọ ni | Pẹ̀lẹ́ o
  - Answer: Ó yá
- Q6 (pick): Who should you use these casual phrases with?
  - Choices: Friends and people your age | Elders you have just met | Anyone, in any situation
  - Answer: Friends and people your age
- Q7 (order): No problem
  - Answer: Kò sí wàhálà
- Q8 (order): You hear?
  - Answer: Ṣé o gbọ́
